# Decisions

## Decisions

| ID | Content | Approach | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DEC-0989 | Change request: .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0001-assistant-routing.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0161/03_Example.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0015-qfai-workflow.md | - Date: 2026-09-28 - Requested by: the routing defaults had grown to 1,203 lines in one file, past the 800-line ceiling of an assistant asset, with more step entries coming from the route catalog. Nothing measured the file: the ceiling covers `.qfai/assistant/**`, and the defaults moved out of that tree into the package. - Change: BR-0001-0027 now says the routing defaults are one list, read in file-name order from every file of the package routing directory (one file per owner), with no step or skill named in two files and no file over the ceiling. EX-0001-0161-07 states the same, and its test measures each file against the doctor line and width ceilings. EX-0001-0161-06 and BR-0015-0044 name the routing defaults instead of the retired single file. No routing entry changed. - Approval: requested on 2026-09-28 within the route catalog work DEC-0988 approves. | DONE |
| DEC-0990 | Change request: .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0186/03_Example.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0214/03_Example.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0215/03_Example.md | - Date: 2026-09-28 - Requested by: three examples described re-routes the route catalog cannot make or a `branch` the stage result schema refuses. - Change: EX-0001-0186-10 described a RED receipt accepted before a re-route out of `fix-defect`, which no route allows because every branch point precedes its implement stage. It now describes a `fix-defect` run whose diagnosis `expectation-differs` re-routes it to `decide-acceptance`, whose `triage-close` reports `adopted` toward `add-feature`; the first `add-feature` work order names the unannotated example in `obligations.ids` and gives `{ ref, validity }` for each of the four prior receipts. EX-0001-0215-10 and EX-0001-0214-04 now show the branch extraction with the four fields the schema requires: `intent`, `entryFlags`, `qualifiers` and `signals`; EX-0001-0214-04 names `fix-defect` as the route the decision rules give it. No business rule changed. - Approval: requested on 2026-09-28 within the route catalog work DEC-0988 approves. | DONE |
| DEC-0991 | Change request: .qfai/spec/01_policy/constraint.md, .qfai/spec/01_policy/glossary.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/tech.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0010-qfai-migration-v1-to-v2.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0014-qfai-validate.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0015-qfai-workflow.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0016-repository-ci.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0019-story-tree-authoring.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0006/02_Acceptance-Criteria.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0006/03_Example.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0039/03_Example.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0051/02_Acceptance-Criteria.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0051/03_Example.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0185/03_Example.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0002/user-story-0002-0014/03_Example.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0004/user-story-0004-0006/03_Example.md | - Date: 2026-09-29 - Requested by: constraint.md mixed policy with concrete detail: file names, commands, rule IDs, and an Impact column pointing at downstream homes, which upstream policy does not reference. Architecture boundaries had no home of their own, and BR-0015-0035 gave the discussion stage `DESIGN.md`, which BR-0007-0007 and BR-0019-0054 give to `/qfai-sdd`. - Change: constraint.md is abstract policy: three sections of ID, Constraint and Rationale, no backtick and no BR, EX, AC or contract ID (BR-0019-0065), and positional IDs, `TC-`, `OC-` or `BC-` from 01 in table order, which `qfai validate` checks as `QFAI-STORY-012` (BR-0014-0106, AC-0001-0051-06). The live file keeps eight rows, renumbered; the rows a contract, tech.md or principle.md already states are removed; the detail of the determinism row becomes BR-0014-0107, the finding-code form BR-0014-0108, and the release workflow file name BR-0016-0079; the conventions on building paths and on catching up with the default branch move to CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md, and the core-to-cli boundary to the Architecture table. tech.md gains a required `## Architecture` table of Layer, Responsibility and Depends on (BR-0019-0066), and the shipped guidance says what it is written from, who reads it and what it does; the constitution routes architecture boundaries there. Migration step 3 writes layer tables into it rather than into constraint.md, drops the Impact column, and numbers each constraint section from 01, listing every changed ID for a person (BR-0010-0077, BR-0010-0078, BR-0010-0080). BR-0015-0035 gives `DESIGN.md` to `sdd` and only the tracked records to `discussion`, and the route proposal check follows it. The glossary's discussion-pack definition no longer restates a rule. - Approval: approved by the user on 2026-09-29. | DONE |

.qfai/spec/open-questions.md | - Date: 2026-09-29 - Requested by: the user, on 2026-09-29. The 2.0.0 tree was rebuilt story by story and is not released yet, and every ID family carried gaps left by retired and moved items. - Change: every ID family was renumbered once to a contiguous sequence, keeping each family's order: CLI contracts to 0001-0020, the business rules of each contract, the stories of each flow, the criteria and examples of each story, the decisions and the open questions. Contract files and story directories were renamed to their new numbers, and every reference in the tree, the tests, the scripts, the workflow comments and the unreleased changelog section was rewritten through one map. The decision rows are written in ascending order. - History rows: an ID a decision or open-question row names was rewritten through the same map. An ID with no current item, and every ID in a row migrated from a 1.x spec pack (whose IDs name 1.x items), now reads as a marker such as `retired US`, `retired AC` or `retired DEC`, which no ID pattern matches; a story directory with no current story reads `user-story-retired`. A row migrated from 1.x keeps a decision it was closed against, since 1.x decision IDs never reached 0100. - Exception: this renumber is the one recorded exception to BR-0019-0014 and BR-0019-0033. Both stand unchanged: from here on a gap is never filled and an ID is never reused. - Approval: decided by the user on 2026-09-29. | DONE |

The repository root had no `qfai` dependency, so the preflight failed and a bare `npx qfai` fetched the published copy. - Change: the root manifest depends on the package as `"qfai": "workspace:*"`. pnpm links `node_modules/.bin/qfai` to `packages/qfai` once `packages/qfai/dist/` exists, so a maintainer runs `pnpm install`, `pnpm build`, then `pnpm install` again on a fresh clone. No install lifecycle script was added: pnpm links the root's binaries before the root's own `postinstall` or `prepare` runs, so a build there still leaves the link missing until the next install. US-0002-0020 and EX-0002-0020-02 said the root manifest declares no dependency on the package. They now give the reason that still holds: the shipped validate workflow installs without building, so the workspace link has no binary and `npx qfai` there would resolve to the published package. BR-0016-0058 is unchanged. - Approval: approved by the user on 2026-09-29. | DONE |
| DEC-1003 | Change request: .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0009-qfai-init.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0196/02_Acceptance-Criteria.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0196/03_Example.md | - Date: 2026-09-29 - Requested by: Codex received only the prompt-time reminders, so the documentation-clarity, grilling, minimal-implementation and API-budget reminders never reached a Codex session at the tool call they are about. - Change: `.codex/hooks.json` carries a group for each Claude Code tool-time reminder that has a Codex moment, with the same markers and message keys, matched on the tool names Codex writes into hook input: `apply_patch`, `spawn_agent`, `Bash` and `mcp__<server>__<tool>` (BR-0009-0063, AC-0001-0196-12, EX-0001-0196-37 to EX-0001-0196-40). Codex hooks have no `if` condition, so the Markdown write and edit entries read the patch from `tool_input.command` and print only for an added or updated `.md` file. BR-0009-0060 no longer says the file holds prompt-time groups only, AC-0001-0196-11 no longer says Codex runs no hook tied to a tool, and EX-0001-0196-26 limits its only-two-groups outcome to `UserPromptSubmit`. Every Codex entry, the prompt-time ones included, also gets a `commandWindows` line (BR-0009-0064, EX-0001-0196-41). Codex runs a hook on Windows through `cmd.exe /C`, where `$(...)` does not expand, so without it every Codex hook printed nothing there. The line finds the git root with `for /f` over `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`, runs the same reader, and ends in `exit /b 0` so a failed lookup still exits 0. This revises DEC-1001, which left `commandWindows` out before that was known. BR-0009-0060 drops the shell that does not expand `$(...)` from the cases that print nothing. The repository's own `.codex/hooks.json` stays identical to the shipped one. - Left out: the plan grilling reminder, because Codex has no tool call that leaves plan mode. - Approval: requested by the user on 2026-09-29. | DONE |

The `$(git rev-parse ...)` form of `command` did not run under PowerShell or `cmd.exe` either. - Change: each entry is one line, `node -e "<program>" <key>`, whose program finds `.agents/rules/reminders.json` itself: it looks in the directory Codex runs it from and in each directory above, and stops after the first that holds `.git` (BR-0009-0060, EX-0001-0196-27). The program holds no character any of `sh`, `cmd.exe` or PowerShell expands, so the same line runs under all of them, and no entry has a `commandWindows` (BR-0009-0064, EX-0001-0196-41). The API-budget filter tests the command with a space added at each end in place of lookarounds, and the Markdown filters are unchanged (BR-0009-0063, EX-0001-0196-38, EX-0001-0196-40). A project below its git root now gets the reminders from its own `.agents/rules`, where it printed nothing before. A Codex group exactly as the previous release wrote it is replaced by this release's on the next `qfai init` (EX-0001-0196-42). The Windows parity job runs the two Codex hook suites, so the `cmd.exe` and PowerShell cases run in CI (BR-0016-0073, EX-0002-0024-01). | DEC-1010 | The Windows parity job's `timeout-minutes` is set from measured runs | - Date: 2026-09-30 - Context: the job was added with an unmeasured ceiling of 30 minutes, and the code-path cost pin (BR-0016-0066) counts that ceiling. - Measurement, per suite: the first run on the default branch, https://github.com/aganesy/QFAI/actions/runs/36134496895 at `b5d357c1` on 2026-09-25. One vitest process ran 146 files and 1,329 tests (1,311 passed, 18 skipped, none failed) in 427.3 s of wall clock. Summed file time per suite-list entry, from vitest's own per-file durations (the files run in parallel, so the sum, 1,591.2 s, exceeds the wall clock): `tests/unit/workflow/` 75 files, 311 tests, 0.8 s; `tests/integration/workflow/` 35 files, 209 tests, 476.7 s, slowest `journal.test.ts` at 124.6 s; `tests/cli/init` 18 files, 630 tests (11 skipped), 954.1 s, slowest `initAgentEntryPointRules.test.ts` at 394.4 s; `tests/integration/init/` 13 files, 31 tests, 112.8 s, slowest `conflictReport.test.ts` at 25.4 s; `tests/core/assistantAssetProvenance.test.ts` 58 tests (7 skipped), 22.0 s; `tests/unit/shared/provenanceLockConfirm.test.ts` 4 tests, 21.8 s; `tests/core/gitignoreGovernanceRecords.test.ts` 20 tests, 2.9 s; `tests/core/gitignoreMatcher.test.ts` 60 tests, 0.03 s; `tests/validators/assistantTreeMigration.test.ts` 6 tests, 0.04 s. One file sets the wall clock: `tests/cli/initAgentEntryPointRules.test.ts` runs for 394.4 s of the 427.3 s. - Measurement, the distribution: the jobs API reports the job on 20 runs from 2026-09-25, every one `success`: three on the pull request that added the job, the default-branch run above, and sixteen on later pull requests. Their trees differ, so the figures describe the job rather than one revision. Min, median and max: the job 317 s, 531 s, 582 s; the setup action with the install 34 s, 42 s, 71 s; the build 9 s, 13.5 s, 16 s; the test step 263 s, 447 s, 506 s. - Decision: `timeout-minutes` is twice the slowest job measured, rounded up to the next five minutes. Twice 582 s is 1,164 s, or 19.4 minutes, so the job declares 20, down from 30. The factor of two covers runner variance, which is already wide: the test step alone spans 263 s to 506 s. - Cost pin: the declared minutes of the code-path pin move from 320 to 310. The instance, install and build-job figures do not change. These are declared ceilings; this record makes no claim that the cost is acceptable. - Shipped CI: not applicable, because the shipped workflow set has no Windows job. - Rejected: keeping 30, a ceiling no run set, which lets a hung Windows job hold a runner for 30 minutes when no measured run took 10. | DONE |

This bounded change was selected by the agent under the user's standing delegation, with no specific human option answer or native workflow run. - Residual risk: The source repair, caller regressions and required owner checks remain pending; writing the specifications alone does not complete this change. - Rollback: Restore only this invocation's criterion and contract clauses and remove EX33 through a separate change request; keep earlier artifacts and decision history. | SUPERSEDED (by DEC-2048) |

.qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0020-workflow-files.md | - Date: 2026-10-04 - Approved by: yusuke_senaga, on 2026-10-04, answering structured questions in the discussion session on how much work each route does; the questions were asked in Japanese, and each chosen option is given here in English: (1) a change route ends with a local commit, and push, pull request and merge stay on the user's own instruction; (2) a route that writes specifications keeps the specification review and the code review, a fix route keeps the code review only, nothing is reviewed twice, and every review the route catalog does not name goes; (3) the diagnosis of a defect is its failing test, a missing example is appended without asking unless it contradicts the specification, and a fix route loses the bisect and minimize pass-throughs, the acceptance scaffold and its separate specification stage; (4) the start-of-stage refresh is renamed policy-check, runs once per run, and the old word and the old-path detection go; (5) grilling records stay in the discussion stage only, a stage reads the rules its step cites, the Work Orders Summary is kept for parallel fan-out only, and the Change Type declaration, the Red test hash, the verify classification block and the long reviewer response go; (6) while implementing only the test being written runs, and lint, typecheck, build, the full tests and qfai validate run once, in the verify stage; (7) fix the contradictions the research found; (8) ship in 2.1.0 - Change: the route catalog of cli-0015-qfai-workflow.md changes these routes: repair-consistency, retire-mechanism, restate-records, add-feature, prototype-feature, change-compatibility, apply-settled, refactor-code, edit-text, fix-defect, improve-performance, fix-vulnerability, fix-intermittent, fix-env-bound, fix-conformance, quarantine-flaky, repair-test, fix-red-main, change-tooling, bump-dependency, revert-culprit, write-acceptance-tests and backport-fix. Every one of them ends its verify stage with verify-commit; no specification stage runs sdd-gate; and fix-defect, improve-performance, fix-vulnerability, fix-intermittent, fix-env-bound, fix-conformance and revert-culprit lose the specification stage, its review and implement-scaffold, fix-defect also losing implement-bisect and implement-minimize - Also changed: a missing example is appended by implement-tdd without asking unless it contradicts the specification, with the one change request the drift gate needs (BR-0015-0072 to BR-0015-0074, BR-0015-0292, BR-0019-0046, BR-0019-0048, BR-0019-0053, BR-0019-0055); the verify block ends with verify-commit (BR-0015-0237, BR-0002-0011, BR-0020-0016, BR-0020-0017); the stage kind sdd_append and the bisect and minimize pass-throughs go (BR-0015-0229, BR-0015-0233, BR-0020-0013, BR-0020-0015); a step's routing entry has no review phase and no blocking agent (BR-0001-0027); Stage 0 is the policy check, run once per run (BR-0004-0003), and the implement skill runs no validate inside a route (BR-0004-0023); the old name of the policy files goes from the current stories and contracts (BR-0003-0001), and the detection of the retired policy directory of the assistant tree goes from init, validate and migration step 12, the legacy instructions directory keeping its own (BR-0009-0017, BR-0009-0020, BR-0009-0033, BR-0009-0035, BR-0010-0110, BR-0010-0112, BR-0014-0021, BR-0014-0088); and the stories and examples that state those rules follow - Added: BR-0015-0298 (a fix diagnosis reproduces with a failing test), BR-0015-0299 (verify-commit), BR-0015-0300 (only the reviews a plan names), BR-0015-0301 (gates once, in verify) and BR-0015-0302 (grilling records only in the discussion stage); EX-0001-0186-14 to EX-0001-0186-16, EX-0001-0225-07 to EX-0001-0225-09 and EX-0001-0223-09 - Retired: BR-0014-0025, AC-0001-0046-01 and EX-0001-0046-01, the check of a skill's reference to a retired assistant path - Why: the user asked that every route do only the work it needs, so that a simple fix is a failing test, the fix, one review and a commit - Supersedes, in part: DEC-2049 for the route rows named above, the append stage and the steps it took off a fix route; The counts are held against the jobs and conditions the file declares by the repository cost reader, so a header cannot go stale. BR-0008-0063, AC-0003-0011-12, EX-0003-0011-27 and EX-0003-0011-28 have qfai doctor report the same counts for the installed shipped workflows as one info check, with the runner selector of each job. Doctor reads no repository variable and uses no network (BR-0008-0027), so it reports the variables a selector falls back through and not the label they resolve to, and it names a workflow it cannot read instead of guessing. BR-0008-0064, AC-0003-0011-13, EX-0003-0011-29 and EX-0003-0011-30 have doctor warn for each YAML file under the workflows and actions directories that cannot be parsed, a key twice in one mapping included, because GitHub starts no job from such a file. The README documents the counts, the required checks, and actionlint as the recommended check after a scripted workflow edit. Left out: a rename of the validation job's check name, which would leave an existing required check unreported in every adopter that already requires it; reading repository variables or the plan; and any change to the jobs the workflows start. The maintainer approved this change in the working session on 2026-10-08, in reply to the coordinator's question, choosing to implement all of the CI cost series; no earlier approval is relied on. - Residual risk: A doctor count is computed from the installed text with the test lane at its bound of five layers, so a project that declares fewer test scripts starts fewer jobs than the upper figure; the condition forms are the shipped ones, so a shipped template that gains a new form is reported as not counted until the reader learns it. A workflow file that GitHub reads but the parser rejects, or the reverse, is reported on the parser's reading. Every project that keeps a file with a repeated key now sees a warning on each doctor run, and under fail-on warning doctor exits 1 until it is fixed. - Rollback: revert the commit, which removes the header rows, the doctor checks, the contract rows, the criteria, the examples, the README text and the tests together. | DONE |

| DEC-2127 | Test exception: AC-0001-0108-02, EX-0001-0108-02 | - Evidence: file:packages/qfai/assets/validators/layoutAntiPatterns.json; file:packages/qfai/src/core/validators/layoutAntiPatterns.ts; file:packages/qfai/assets/init/.qfai/assistant/skill/qfai-prototyping/references/reviewer-prompt.md; file:packages/qfai/assets/init/.qfai/assistant/skill/qfai-prototyping/references/review-payload-schema.md; command:node scripts/check-dogfood-backlog.mjs --profile full - Grounds: the maintainer decided in the working session on 2026-10-09, in reply to the coordinator's question, to record obligations that no test can prove as Test exception rows grouped by reason; no earlier approval is relied on. The reason for this group is that the obligation concerns what a reviewing agent writes into its review payload at run time, and neither code nor a shipped text states it, so no test can observe it. AC-0001-0108-02 and EX-0001-0108-02 require that `layoutAntiPatternsDetected[]` holds only identifiers the registry declares and that a problem the registry does not declare is named in a blocking finding instead. No source file reads a review payload or compares its `layoutAntiPatternsDetected[]` with the registry: the registry loader only reads the registry and matches its regular expressions against iteration HTML. The shipped reviewer prompt tells the reviewer to apply the registry and the payload schema types the field as identifiers from the registry, but neither text carries the second clause, so a text assertion would prove only the first half and leave the blocking-finding half to the reviewer's judgement. The two IDs are exempt for the same reason, each for its own obligation: the acceptance criterion for the recording rule, the example for the undeclared-problem case. Left out: AC-0001-0108-01 and EX-0001-0108-01, whose score cap the shipped prompt states and a test can pin. - Residual risk: a reviewer may record an identifier the registry does not declare, or omit the blocking finding, and nothing in CI notices; the review that reads the payload is the only check. - Rollback: remove this row and add the test for each ID, which first needs a payload reader in the code or the second clause in the shipped prompt. | DONE |

Wording revised to the shipped behaviour: US-0001-0012 and BR-0009-0002 (an asset is placed by the tree that owns it: rule detail under rule/references, a skill's detail under that skill's references, and no reader count decides it, since skill references are cited across skills and the CLI reads files in both places), US-0001-0067 (no volume estimator exists; qfai atdd scaffold writes one skeleton per BF and per AC), US-0001-0072 and BR-0004-0007 (depth is read by test-design-analyst and qa-gatekeeper, which return REVISE where an error, boundary or edge case is meaningful and invent none; the two reviewers the criterion named do not read it), US-0001-0084 (the reviewer prompt's example is a checkout action below the fold), US-0001-0091 (implement takes the Test command and verify takes Lint, Typecheck and Build, all from the Standard commands section, and a missing entry is UNRUN), US-0001-0157 and BR-0004-0027 (a REVISE blocks until each finding is fixed or answered, with no re-review), US-0001-0164, US-0001-0166, BR-0001-0005 and BR-0001-0008 (the verdicts are PASS and REVISE and there is no FAIL), US-0001-0171 and BR-0001-0013 (the schema field is primaryUiContract; QFAI-HANDOFF-001 reports an asymmetric edit of the schema and its writers, and the saas-package profile reports a non-conforming prototyping record). Shipped assets fixed: section 2 of minimal-implementation.md no longer names an execution ledger, as BR-0004-0021 requires, and references/context-load.md reads paths.specsDir, paths.contractsDir and principle.md as BR-0004-0032 requires, in place of fixed paths. - Residual risk: the depth and fix obligations rest on the text of the reviewer cards and the delegation baseline, which no run can enforce; the verify context step now names principle.md, which a project may not have, and the step reads policy files only if present. - Rollback: revert the commit, which restores the criteria, the contract rows, the two assets, the test changes and the pins in scripts/dogfood-backlog.json. | PARTLY SUPERSEDED (by DEC-2274) |

The reason for this group is that the obligation is a rule for how a parallelism tuning change is made, reviewed and merged by people and agents, and its final clauses concern text on a pull request and the history of CI runs after a merge, which no file in the repository holds. AC-0002-0019-03 requires the timings to be quoted in the pull-request description and a revision of the declared starting value to carry the user's sign-off. AC-0002-0019-04 requires each tuning change to land on its own pull request with three consecutive green runs recorded before merge, and a rerun-to-green rate above one in twenty default-branch runs afterwards to reopen the setting. What a tree can show is already asserted at the example level: the adopted worker value against the recorded measurement (EX-0002-0019-03), the flakier-setting outcome (EX-0002-0019-04), the refused revision (EX-0002-0019-05), the retry-free workspace (EX-0002-0019-06), one moved project at a time (EX-0002-0019-07), the run identifiers recorded for a move (EX-0002-0019-12) and the post-merge budget predicate (EX-0002-0019-08). Those examples keep their own annotations. The two acceptance criteria are exempt for the same reason, each for its own obligation: the first for the pull-request text and the sign-off, the second for the per-pull-request cadence and the post-merge rerun rate. - Residual risk: a tuning change may merge without timings in its description or without three green runs behind it, and a high rerun rate may go unnoticed; the reviewer of that pull request and the default-branch run history are the only checks. - Rollback: remove this row and add the test for each ID, which first needs the pull-request text and the CI run history readable from the repository. | DONE |
